# How Much Does a Parking Automation System Cost in Australia?

One of the first questions operators ask when considering an upgrade is simple: how much does a parking automation system actually cost?

The honest answer is that pricing can vary significantly depending on the size of your site, the type of technology you choose and how the system is configured. What’s important is understanding what drives the cost and how modern systems can often reduce long-term expenses compared to traditional setups.

Here’s a clear breakdown of what influences the cost of a parking automation system in Australia and what you should factor in before making a decision.

## What Impacts the Cost of a Parking Automation System?

No two car parks are the same, which is why pricing is rarely one-size-fits-all. Several key factors influence the overall investment.

### Number of Entry and Exit Points

The more entry and exit lanes your site has, the more hardware and configuration is required. A small single-entry car park will naturally cost less than a large retail or hospital site with multiple access points.

### Type of Technology Used

Traditional ticket-based systems typically require:

- Ticket dispensers

- Payment machines

- Barriers

- Ongoing maintenance

Modern parking automation systems often rely on ANPR cameras and cloud software instead, reducing the need for bulky hardware. While the upfront cost can differ, automated systems often cost less to maintain over time.

## ANPR vs Traditional Parking Systems

ANPR-based parking systems use licence plate recognition to manage access and payment. These systems remove the need for paper tickets and reduce the number of physical components on site.

From a cost perspective, ANPR systems often:

- Lower maintenance expenses

- Reduce downtime

- Improve revenue accuracy

- Scale more easily across multiple sites

Parkezi specialises in ANPR-powered parking systems that balance upfront investment with long-term savings.

## Installation and Setup Costs

Installation costs depend on:

- Site layout

- Existing infrastructure

- Power and network availability

- Hardware mounting requirements

Some sites require minimal work, while others need civil or electrical adjustments. A proper site assessment is the best way to understand installation costs accurately.

Parkezi provides tailored installations to suit each site, helping operators avoid unnecessary expenses.

## Ongoing Costs to Consider

Beyond installation, it’s important to factor in ongoing costs such as:

- Software access

- System monitoring

- Hardware servicing

- Support and updates

One of the benefits of cloud-based parking systems is predictable ongoing costs and fewer surprise repairs. Systems with fewer moving parts generally require less maintenance and result in lower long-term spend.

## Purchase vs Lease Models

Some operators prefer to purchase their system outright, while others opt for a lease or subscription model. Each approach has advantages.

Purchasing:

- Higher upfront cost

- Lower ongoing fees

Leasing:

- Lower initial outlay

- Predictable monthly costs

- Easier upgrades over time

Parkezi offers flexible [pricing options for parking](https://www.parkezi.com.au/pricing/) depending on what suits your business model and budget.

## Understanding the Real Return on Investment

The true cost of a parking automation system isn’t just what you pay upfront. It’s also what you save.

Many operators see ROI through:

- Reduced staffing requirements

- Lower maintenance costs

- Faster vehicle throughput

- Fewer disputes and revenue errors

- Improved customer satisfaction

When viewed over several years, modern parking automation systems often deliver strong financial and operational returns.

‍

## FAQs

**1. Is there a standard price for a parking automation system in Australia?**

There’s no single standard price, as every site is different and costs depend on factors like the size of the car park, the number of entry and exit points and how much existing infrastructure can be reused. Entry-level systems start from under $10,000, including an ANPR camera and boom gate and typically range from $50,000 - $60,000 for a two-way paid parking management system.

**2. Is an ANPR-based system more expensive than a traditional ticket system?**

Upfront costs can be similar, but ANPR systems often cost less over time. They typically require less maintenance, have fewer breakdowns and reduce manual handling, which can lead to better long-term value.

**3. What ongoing costs should I expect after installation?**

Ongoing costs may include software access, support and occasional hardware servicing. Cloud-based systems usually have predictable costs and fewer unexpected repairs compared to older ticket-based systems.

**4. Can parking automation systems be leased instead of purchased?**

Yes. Many operators choose a lease or subscription model to reduce upfront costs and spread investment over time. [Parkezi offers](https://www.parkezi.com.au/pricing/) flexible options depending on your operational and budget requirements.

**5. How long does it take to see a return on investment?**

This varies by site, but many operators see ROI relatively quickly through reduced maintenance costs, improved traffic flow and more accurate revenue collection.

‍

### Ready to Understand What Parking Automation Would Cost for Your Site?

Every car park is different, and the best way to understand costs is to look at your site’s layout, traffic patterns and operational needs. Whether you’re running a small private car park or managing multiple locations, the right system should work for your budget as well as your users.

Parkezi helps Australian operators assess their sites and recommend practical parking automation solutions that balance upfront cost with long-term value.

[**Get in touch with the Parkezi team**](https://www.parkezi.com.au/contact/) to discuss your requirements and receive tailored advice for your facility.

The true cost of a parking automation system isn’t just what you pay upfront. It’s also what you save.
Many operators see ROI through:
Reduced staffing requirements
Lower maintenance costs
Faster vehicle throughput
Fewer disputes and revenue errors
Improved customer satisfaction
When viewed over several years, modern parking automation systems often deliver strong financial and operational returns.
FAQs
1. Is there a standard price for a parking automation system in Australia?
There’s no single standard price, as every site is different and costs depend on factors like the size of the car park, the number of entry and exit points and how much existing infrastructure can be reused. Entry-level systems start from under $10,000, including an ANPR camera and boom gate and typically range from $50,000 - $60,000 for a two-way paid parking management system.
2. Is an ANPR-based system more expensive than a traditional ticket system?
Upfront costs can be similar, but ANPR systems often cost less over time. They typically require less maintenance, have fewer breakdowns and reduce manual handling, which can lead to better long-term value.
3. What ongoing costs should I expect after installation?
Ongoing costs may include software access, support and occasional hardware servicing. Cloud-based systems usually have predictable costs and fewer unexpected repairs compared to older ticket-based systems.
4. Can parking automation systems be leased instead of purchased?
Yes. Many operators choose a lease or subscription model to reduce upfront costs and spread investment over time.
Parkezi offers
flexible options depending on your operational and budget requirements.
5. How long does it take to see a return on investment?
This varies by site, but many operators see ROI relatively quickly through reduced maintenance costs, improved traffic flow and more accurate revenue collection.
Discuss your site
Every car park is different, and the best way to understand costs is to look at your site’s layout, traffic patterns and operational needs. Whether you’re running a small private car park or managing multiple locations, the right system should work for your budget as well as your users.
Parkezi helps Australian operators assess their sites and recommend practical parking automation solutions that balance upfront cost with long-term value.

to discuss your requirements and receive tailored advice for your facility.

# How Ticketless Parking Improves Customer Experience and Revenue

If there’s one thing drivers want today, it’s convenience. From shopping to dining to transport, people expect quick and simple ways to move through their day. Parking is no different. As more Australians rely on mobile payments and automated access, operators are moving away from outdated machines and embracing ticketless parking systems.

Ticketless and contactless parking refer to related but distinct aspects of the modern parking experience. Ticketless parking removes the need for a physical paper ticket altogether, while contactless parking removes physical touchpoints such as cash handling, card insertion or interacting with payment machines. Together, they create a smoother, faster and more reliable experience for both drivers and car park operators.

This shift isn’t just about keeping up with expectations. It’s also helping operators reduce costs, ease congestion and improve revenue accuracy. Here’s how ticketless parking systems are delivering real benefits across Australian car parks.

‍

**A Faster, Smoother Entry and Exit for Drivers**

In a traditional car park, the friction starts at the entry. Drivers roll up, stop, press a button, wait for a ticket to print, then take it and keep it safe for later. If they drop it, misplace it, or it gets damaged, the exit becomes a headache — and it usually happens when they’re already in a rush.

Then at the other end, the exit is often where queues build. Drivers pull up, insert a ticket (sometimes the wrong way), wait for it to read, and hope they’re still within the free period. If the ticket doesn’t scan properly or they’ve overstayed, the whole lane can stall while payment is sorted out.

ParkEzi removes that friction by taking tickets out of the equation. Using licence plate recognition, vehicles can be identified automatically, so there’s no need to stop, press buttons, or handle paper tickets at entry.

At the exit, the screen displays an image of the vehicle so the driver can clearly confirm the correct car has been scanned. If the stay is within the free period, the boom gate opens automatically — no ticket handling, no “bad read” delays, and no guessing.

If payment is required, it’s still quick. Drivers can pay right there using contactless tap-to-pay, either with a credit card or a digital wallet. A QR code on the screen can also be scanned to access a digital receipt instantly.

For drivers, this means:

- Smoother entry with no button pressing or ticket collection

- No paper tickets to lose, damage, or insert the wrong way

- Faster exits, especially during peak periods

- Vehicle image confirmation at the exit screen for peace of mind

- Simple contactless payment when needed

- Instant access to a digital receipt via QR code

ParkEzi’s Express Pay and Scan & Go experience is designed to keep cars moving and make parking feel effortless from entry to exit.

## Reduced Congestion and Shorter Wait Times

One of the biggest causes of congestion in car parks is the exit bottleneck. When every driver has to stop, tap, wait and then drive off, queues are inevitable.

Ticketless and contactless systems spread the payment process over time. Drivers can pay at their convenience, while walking back to their car or automatically as they exit. This keeps vehicles moving and reduces pressure at exit points.

The result is:

- Less idle time

- Smoother vehicle flow

- Shorter queues during peak periods

For high-traffic environments such as shopping centres, hospitals and hotels, this difference is immediately noticeable.

## Lower Operating Costs for Car Park Owners

Traditional parking machines are costly to purchase and maintain. They require regular servicing, paper supplies, card reader repairs and frequent cleaning. Cash handling also introduces extra labour and security costs.

Ticketless parking systems streamline operations by reducing the amount of physical hardware on site. With fewer moving parts, there are fewer breakdowns and less maintenance required.

Operators benefit from:

- Reduced servicing costs

- Fewer equipment failures

- Less on-site intervention

- Lower long-term hardware expenses

Parkezi’s ticketless platforms are designed to minimise ongoing costs while improving reliability and performance.

## More Accurate and Reliable Revenue Collection

Manual systems leave room for error. Tickets can be misread, machines can malfunction and transactions can go missing. Ticketless parking relies on digital verification, which significantly reduces these risks.

With cloud-based systems, every entry, exit and payment is logged in real time. Operators can access reports instantly and rely on accurate data for reconciliations and audits.

This leads to:

- Less revenue leakage

- Clearer audit trails

- More transparent financial reporting

Parkezi’s reporting tools provide clear visibility into revenue and occupancy trends, helping operators make informed decisions with confidence.

## Flexible Payment Options That Suit Every Driver

One of the biggest advantages of ticketless and contactless parking is flexibility. Different drivers prefer different payment methods, and modern systems are designed to support that.

With Parkezi, drivers can choose to pay using:

- Tap-and-go cards

- Apple Pay or Google Pay

- QR code web payments

- Mobile checkout links

- Automatic licence plate payments

Giving drivers choice removes confusion and reduces friction. When parking feels easy, people are far more likely to return.

## A More Modern and Trustworthy Image

Car parks are often the first and last interaction people have with a site. A smooth, ticketless experience feels modern and well managed. Long queues, broken machines and paper tickets create the opposite impression.

By upgrading to ticketless and contactless parking, operators show they value efficiency, convenience and customer experience. This strengthens trust and improves how the entire site is perceived.

Parkezi works with operators across Australia to modernise car parks using simple, user-friendly ticketless solutions that improve both satisfaction and revenue.

## FAQs

### 1. What exactly is ticketless parking and how does it work?

Ticketless parking uses licence plate recognition to manage entry, exit and payment without issuing a physical ticket. Drivers can pay digitally, often through their phone or automatically, making the process faster and more convenient.

### 2. How is ticketless parking different from contactless parking?

Ticketless parking removes the need for a paper ticket, while contactless parking removes physical touchpoints such as cash or card insertion. Most modern systems combine both to create a seamless experience.

### 3. Does ticketless parking really improve the customer experience?

Yes. Drivers spend less time waiting, queues are shorter and there’s no stress around lost tickets or broken machines. This makes parking feel easier and more reliable.

### 4. Is ticketless parking suitable for busy locations?

Absolutely. High-traffic locations often see the biggest improvements, as faster flow and distributed payment reduce congestion during peak periods.

### 5. Do drivers still get a choice in how they pay?

Yes. Flexibility is a key benefit. Drivers can choose from multiple digital payment options, which makes the system more accessible and user friendly.

## Why Ticketless Parking Is the Way Forward

If your car park is still relying on paper tickets or ageing payment machines, it may be time to rethink how drivers move through your site. Ticketless parking is no longer an added extra. It’s what customers expect.

If you’d like to explore how ticketless and contactless parking could work for your location, the team at Parkezi is always happy to help you find the right solution. [Contact Parkezi today](https://www.parkezi.com.au/contact/) for your ticketless parking installation.

‍

Before: captured original

# Contactless vs Traditional Parking Systems: Which Is Right for Your Site?

Car parks have changed dramatically over the past decade. Where paper tickets and cash payments were once the norm, drivers now expect quick, contact-free experiences that match the rest of their daily transactions.

If you’re reviewing your current setup, you might be weighing up whether to stick with a traditional system or move to a contactless parking solution. Understanding the differences can help you decide what’s right for your site.

## What Is a Traditional Parking System?

Traditional parking systems typically rely on:

- Paper tickets

- Physical payment machines

- Cash or card insertion

- Manual troubleshooting

While these systems still operate in many locations, they often struggle during peak times and require regular maintenance to keep running smoothly.

## What Is a Contactless Parking System?

[Contactless parking systems](https://www.parkezi.com.au/products/scan-and-go/) remove the need for physical interaction with machines. Payments are handled digitally, and access is often managed using ANPR cameras and cloud software.

Drivers can pay using:

- Tap-and-go cards

- Mobile wallets

- QR codes

- Web-based checkout

This approach reduces touchpoints and keeps traffic moving.

## Entry and Exit Speed

Traditional systems slow traffic down. Drivers must stop, collect tickets, find machines and queue at exits.

Contactless systems streamline this process. Payments can be completed before drivers reach the exit, or handled automatically, which significantly reduces congestion during busy periods.

## Maintenance and Reliability

Traditional systems rely on multiple mechanical components that wear out over time. Card readers jam, printers fail and screens break.

Contactless systems reduce reliance on physical hardware. Fewer moving parts mean:

- Less downtime

- Lower servicing costs

- Fewer customer complaints

[Parkezi’s contactless solutions](https://www.parkezi.com.au/products/scan-and-go/) are designed to operate reliably with minimal intervention.

## Customer Experience

From a driver’s perspective, contactless parking is simply easier. There’s no stress about lost tickets or broken machines, and the process feels familiar because it mirrors how people already pay for most things.

This improves satisfaction and makes drivers more likely to return.

## Long-Term Cost Comparison

While traditional systems may appear cheaper initially, ongoing maintenance, repairs and manual handling often add up.

Contactless systems typically offer:

- Lower long-term costs

- Better scalability

- Improved revenue accuracy

Over time, many operators find the total cost of ownership is lower with a modern contactless setup.

## Which System Is Right for You?

Traditional systems may still suit small, low-traffic sites with minimal operational demands. However, for busy locations such as shopping centres, hospitals, hotels and commercial precincts, contactless systems offer clear advantages.

Parkezi works with operators across Australia to assess site needs and recommend the most practical solution, whether that’s a full contactless upgrade or a staged transition.

‍

## FAQs

**1. What does contactless parking actually mean?**

Contactless parking removes the need for physical interaction with payment machines. Drivers can pay using mobile wallets, tap-and-go cards, QR codes or web-based checkout, without inserting cash or cards.

**2. Is contactless parking the same as ticketless parking?**

They are related but not the same. Ticketless parking removes the need for a physical paper ticket, while contactless parking removes physical touchpoints during payment. Many modern systems combine both.

**3. Are contactless parking systems secure?**

Yes. Reputable systems use encrypted transactions, secure cloud platforms and role-based access controls. This helps protect both customer data and operator revenue.

**4. Will older drivers struggle with contactless parking?**

Most drivers adapt quickly because contactless parking uses familiar payment methods like cards and smartphones. Clear signage and simple instructions also help ensure a smooth transition.

**5. Can contactless parking work in high-traffic environments?**

Absolutely. Busy sites often benefit the most because faster payment and smoother exits reduce congestion and queues during peak periods.

‍

### Thinking About Upgrading Your Parking System?

If your current parking setup is causing queues, maintenance issues or customer complaints, it may be time to explore a more modern approach. Contactless parking systems offer faster flow, lower maintenance and a smoother experience for drivers.

Parkezi works with car park operators across Australia to help them transition from traditional systems to smarter, contactless solutions without unnecessary disruption.

[**Contact Parkezi today**](https://www.parkezi.com.au/contact/) to talk through your options and find the right parking system for your site.

# Five Common Parking Management Challenges (and How ANPR Solves Them)

Managing a car park might look straightforward from the outside, but operators know it’s anything but simple. Between lost tickets, peak-time bottlenecks, frustrated drivers and manual processes that chew up hours of staff time, traditional parking systems come with a long list of daily challenges.

The good news is that modern ANPR technology is helping Australian car parks overcome these issues without extra staffing or complicated equipment. If you’ve been wondering whether ANPR can genuinely improve operations, here’s a breakdown of the challenges it solves every single day.

‍

## 1. Lost Tickets and Slow Queues at the Gate

Anyone who has worked in car park operations knows the pain of lost or damaged tickets. They disrupt flow, frustrate customers and create unnecessary manual intervention.

ANPR removes the issue completely. When a vehicle enters, the camera reads the licence plate instantly. When the driver leaves, the system simply matches the plate to the visit and calculates the fee.

That means:

- No paper

- No fumbling at machines

- No window-down interactions at exit points

- Much faster peak-time movement

Parkezi’s ANPR-driven systems make the entire entry and exit experience smoother for drivers and easier for operators.

‍

## 2. Revenue Leakage and Manual Error

Traditional parking systems rely heavily on manual inputs. This opens the door to miscounts, inaccurate reconciliations and human error.

ANPR systems create a clean digital record of every entry and exit. This reduces disputes, protects revenue and gives operators reliable data to work with.

You get:

- Accurate timestamps

- Automated matching of stays

- Clear audit trails

- Fewer discrepancies

Parkezi’s cloud platform strengthens this even further by storing and processing all vehicle data securely and in real time.

‍

## 3. Unauthorised Access to Restricted Areas

Swipe cards can be shared. Tickets can be misused. Boom gates can be tailgated. Keeping unauthorised vehicles out of restricted car parks is a challenge for councils, apartments and commercial buildings.

With ANPR access control, operators can set up approved lists, visitor permissions and temporary access rules. If the number plate isn’t recognised, the gate simply doesn’t open.

This makes ANPR one of the most secure and reliable access control systems for:

- Residential and strata car parks

- Staff-only areas

- Visitor bays

- Loading docks

- Private facilities

Parkezi’s access control settings help operators manage permissions without needing to hand out physical passes or cards.

‍

## 4. Limited Visibility Across Sites

If you manage more than one car park, you know how challenging it can be to keep track of occupancy, customer patterns and issues across multiple locations.

ANPR combined with cloud software gives operators an instant snapshot of what’s happening in real time.

You can see:

- Occupancy levels

- Peak demand patterns

- Repeat visits

- Vehicle flow issues

- Revenue insights

Parkezi’s dashboards give operators a clear view across single or multi-site portfolios, making planning and reporting much easier.

‍

## 5. High Maintenance and Downtime from Old Hardware

Older ticket machines, card readers and payment kiosks wear out quickly. They need costly servicing and often break down during busy periods, which leads to frustrated customers and lost revenue.

ANPR dramatically reduces the amount of physical hardware on-site. Fewer moving parts means less maintenance, lower replacement costs and more uptime.

Ticketless systems also:

- Reduce paper waste

- Remove the need for magnetic readers

- Cut down on servicing

- Eliminate many of the common failure points

Parkezi’s ticketless platforms are designed to run with minimal intervention, which means operators can focus on service and strategy rather than troubleshooting equipment.

‍

## Why ANPR Is the Smarter Way Forward

ANPR isn’t just a fancy feature. It’s now one of the most practical and cost-effective tools for improving the daily operations of a car park. Whether you run a retail centre, hotel, apartment building or council facility, the efficiency gains are immediate.

By automating access, reducing manual tasks and improving visibility, ANPR helps operators:

- Reduce congestion

- Improve customer satisfaction

- Strengthen security

- Boost revenue accuracy

- Modernise the entire parking experience

Parkezi brings all of these elements together with a smart parking management system built for real-world Australian conditions.

‍

## Ready to Upgrade? Let Parkezi Help

If you’re looking for a simpler, smarter and more secure way to manage your car park, ANPR might be the solution you’ve been waiting for. Parkezi can help you transition to a ticketless, cloud-based system that removes the everyday headaches of traditional parking setups.

Explore how Parkezi’s ANPR-powered parking solutions can make life easier for both your team and your customers. [Contact us today.](https://www.parkezi.com.au/contact)

post/how-anpr-improves-security-in-commercial-and-residential-car-parks article

Source body/lead retained with current links and shorter headings.

Before: captured original

# How ANPR Improves Security in Commercial and Residential Car Parks

Car park security usually becomes a priority after something goes wrong. An unauthorised vehicle gains access. A swipe card is shared. A resident questions whether the building is truly secure. What seemed like a simple gate setup suddenly feels inadequate.

For residential complexes, office buildings and commercial facilities, vehicle access control is just as important as front-door security. Traditional systems like remotes, swipe cards and permits have been around for decades, but they all rely on physical credentials that can be lost, copied or misused.

That’s where ANPR, or Automatic Number Plate Recognition, offers a smarter alternative.

Instead of giving access to whoever is holding a card, ANPR grants access to a specific vehicle. That small shift makes a significant difference.

## Why Traditional Access Systems Create Risk

Swipe cards feel convenient until one is duplicated. Remotes are simple until they go missing. Permits work until someone forgets to update them.

Most traditional systems depend on something physical. And anything physical can be shared.

In residential buildings, residents often lend access devices to friends or family. In commercial sites, former staff can still have working credentials long after they leave.

Even manned entry points are not immune. During busy periods, tailgating becomes common and staff simply cannot monitor every vehicle.

Over time, these small gaps weaken overall site security.

## How ANPR Strengthens Control Without Adding Complexity

ANPR works by reading a vehicle’s licence plate as it approaches the gate. The system checks the plate against an approved list and grants or denies access instantly.

There is no card to scan. No permit to display. No button to press.

Access rules are managed digitally through a central platform, which gives property managers far greater control.

With an ANPR parking system, managers can:

- Add or remove vehicles instantly

- Set temporary permissions for visitors

- Monitor entry and exit activity

- Maintain secure access logs

If you are exploring how this works in practice, you can learn more about Parkezi’s ANPR parking systems here:
[https://www.parkezi.com.au/systems/](https://www.parkezi.com.au/systems/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

## Clear Digital Records When You Need Them

Security is not just about stopping unauthorised access. It is also about accountability.

ANPR automatically logs every vehicle entry and exit. If there is a dispute, an incident or even a billing query, there is clear, time-stamped data to refer to.

For strata managers, this provides peace of mind. For commercial operators, it adds transparency.

Instead of relying solely on CCTV or manual reporting, you have structured data supporting every movement.

## Visitor Access Without Compromising Safety

Visitors and contractors are part of daily operations. The challenge is allowing access without weakening security.

ANPR makes this simple. Temporary permissions can be assigned to specific licence plates for defined timeframes. Once that timeframe ends, access is revoked automatically.

There is no need to chase down passes or worry about retrieving access cards.

This balance between flexibility and control is one of the main reasons residential and commercial operators across Australia are upgrading their parking access control systems.

## Reducing Tailgating and Unauthorised Entry

Tailgating remains one of the most common car park security concerns. One vehicle enters legitimately, and another slips through behind it.

While no system is perfect, ANPR integrated with properly configured boom gates reduces this risk significantly. Some systems can even flag irregular entry patterns for review.

When combined with modern access control hardware, the result is a far more secure environment.

## Security That Feels Effortless

The strength of ANPR is that it improves security without making access harder for authorised users.

Residents do not need to remember cards. Staff do not need to manually check lists. Visitors do not need to collect passes.

The system operates quietly in the background.

Parkezi works with property developers, strata managers and commercial operators to design access control setups that suit each site’s layout and traffic needs.

If you are reviewing your current access system, it may also be worth exploring how cloud-based management supports ongoing visibility and control across your facility:
[https://www.parkezi.com.au/](https://www.parkezi.com.au/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### FAQs

###### 1. Is ANPR suitable for small apartment complexes?

Yes. ANPR systems can be scaled to suit both small residential sites and large commercial precincts.

###### 2. What happens if a resident changes vehicles?

The number plate can be updated instantly within the system without replacing any hardware.

###### 3. Does ANPR comply with Australian privacy laws?

Reputable providers ensure that data is encrypted and managed in line with Australian privacy regulations.

###### 4. Can ANPR integrate with existing boom gates?

In many cases, yes. A site assessment will determine compatibility.

‍

### Ready to Strengthen Your Car Park Security?

If you are relying on swipe cards or manual permits, now may be the right time to review your setup. ANPR offers stronger control, clearer visibility and fewer physical vulnerabilities.

To see how a secure, ANPR-based system could work for your property, speak with the team at Parkezi and explore your options.

post/how-cloud-parking-management-software-simplifies-multi-site-operations article

# How Cloud Parking Management Software Simplifies Multi-Site Operations

Managing one car park is straightforward. Managing several across different locations is where complexity begins.

Without a central system, operators often rely on separate reports, inconsistent pricing structures and limited visibility into what is actually happening on each site.

Cloud parking management software brings everything together.

Instead of operating in silos, every location connects to one central dashboard.

## One View Across Every Site

Cloud-based systems allow operators to log in and see occupancy, revenue and system status across all sites in real time.

There is no waiting for manual reports. No piecing together spreadsheets from different managers.

If one site consistently reaches capacity while another sits underutilised, that becomes visible immediately. Decisions can then be made based on facts rather than assumptions.

You can explore how this works in practice through Parkezi’s cloud-based parking management systems here:
[https://www.parkezi.com.au/systems/](https://www.parkezi.com.au/systems/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

## Consistency Across Your Portfolio

Multi-site operators often struggle with standardisation. Different sites may use different pricing rules, access settings or reporting formats.

Cloud systems solve this by allowing central updates.

If pricing changes are required, they can be applied instantly. If access permissions need adjustment, they can be rolled out across multiple sites from one interface.

This is particularly valuable for councils, retail groups and hospital networks where consistency is critical.

## Remote Monitoring and Faster Support

Cloud platforms allow operators to monitor performance without being physically present.

Minor configuration changes and diagnostics can often be handled remotely, reducing downtime and unnecessary service visits.

Parkezi’s cloud-connected systems are designed to give operators visibility and control without adding operational complexity.

## Smarter Decisions Backed by Real Data

With centralised data, operators can analyse:

- Peak traffic periods

- Average dwell times

- Revenue per bay

- Seasonal demand changes

This insight supports smarter planning and better allocation of resources.

It also feeds into broader parking analytics and reporting strategies, helping operators continuously improve performance.

## Scalable for Growth

When new sites are added, cloud systems simplify onboarding. Settings can be duplicated, pricing structures standardised and reporting centralised quickly.

This removes much of the friction typically associated with expansion.

For growing portfolios, cloud-based management is often the only practical way to maintain control without increasing administrative burden.

### FAQs

###### 1. Is cloud parking software secure?

Yes. Leading providers use encrypted platforms and secure hosting environments to protect data.

###### 2. Can multiple managers access the system?

Yes. Role-based access allows different team members to manage or view specific areas.

###### 3. Does cloud software work for small operators?

Absolutely. Even smaller portfolios benefit from central visibility and simplified reporting.

###### 4. What happens if internet connectivity drops?

Most modern systems include safeguards and temporary offline functionality to maintain operation.

### Thinking About Simplifying Your Multi-Site Operations?

If managing multiple car parks feels more complex than it should be, cloud-based parking management may be the solution.

Parkezi works with Australian operators to implement scalable, cloud-connected systems that improve visibility and reduce operational strain.

To see how centralised management could support your sites, visit: [https://www.parkezi.com.au/](https://www.parkezi.com.au/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

Cloud-based systems allow operators to log in and see occupancy, revenue and system status across all sites in real time.
There is no waiting for manual reports. No piecing together spreadsheets from different managers.
If one site consistently reaches capacity while another sits underutilised, that becomes visible immediately. Decisions can then be made based on facts rather than assumptions.
You can explore how this works in practice through Parkezi’s cloud-based parking management systems here:
https://www.parkezi.com.au/systems/
Consistency Across Your Portfolio
Multi-site operators often struggle with standardisation. Different sites may use different pricing rules, access settings or reporting formats.
Cloud systems solve this by allowing central updates.
If pricing changes are required, they can be applied instantly. If access permissions need adjustment, they can be rolled out across multiple sites from one interface.
This is particularly valuable for councils, retail groups and hospital networks where consistency is critical.
Remote Monitoring and Faster Support
Cloud platforms allow operators to monitor performance without being physically present.
Minor configuration changes and diagnostics can often be handled remotely, reducing downtime and unnecessary service visits.
Parkezi’s cloud-connected systems are designed to give operators visibility and control without adding operational complexity.
Smarter Decisions Backed by Real Data
With centralised data, operators can analyse:
Peak traffic periods
Average dwell times
Revenue per bay
Seasonal demand changes
This insight supports smarter planning and better allocation of resources.
It also feeds into broader parking analytics and reporting strategies, helping operators continuously improve performance.
Scalable for Growth
When new sites are added, cloud systems simplify onboarding. Settings can be duplicated, pricing structures standardised and reporting centralised quickly.
This removes much of the friction typically associated with expansion.
For growing portfolios, cloud-based management is often the only practical way to maintain control without increasing administrative burden.
FAQs
1. Is cloud parking software secure?
Yes. Leading providers use encrypted platforms and secure hosting environments to protect data.
2. Can multiple managers access the system?
Yes. Role-based access allows different team members to manage or view specific areas.
3. Does cloud software work for small operators?
Absolutely. Even smaller portfolios benefit from central visibility and simplified reporting.
4. What happens if internet connectivity drops?
Most modern systems include safeguards and temporary offline functionality to maintain operation.
Discuss your sites
If managing multiple car parks feels more complex than it should be, cloud-based parking management may be the solution.
Parkezi works with Australian operators to implement scalable, cloud-connected systems that improve visibility and reduce operational strain.

# How Do Boom Gates Work?

As a driver these days, you’d be hard pressed to go anywhere without going though a boom gate as a first point of security.  Their implementation is commonplace in almost every modern infrastructure project and setting and continues to grow.

‍

## We see boom gates at:

- Shopping centers

- Private and public car parks

- Government buildings

- Businesses, and

- A wide range of other locations, including beaches and parks.

Boom gates have come a long way over the years with technology expanding their efficiency and providing an affordable and convenient alternative to traditional gates and fences.

The right boom gate can also be integrated with other systems, such as automatic number plate recognition (Parkezi) and paperless ticketing, providing an all-inclusive parking access solution making entry and exit from car parks and driveways so much faster and smoother for vehicular traffic.

There’s no doubt that boom gates have changed our lives, but have you ever considered how they work?

A boom gate, often also called a boom barrier, is operated by a geared motor which controls the gradual movement up and down of the pole or arm.  The pole is counter weighted to support the distribution of weight.

For safe operation, most automatic boom barriers have incorporated inductive loops.  The control system safety circuits are linked to ground loops which are located within the road surface.

‍

## What does this mean?

Simply, it prevents boom arms suddenly lowering when vehicles are in the passage area.

Quality boom gates are also designed with the odd accidental knock in mind and feature a wide variety of enhancements and safety built-ins to suit location and traffic.  When designed as a complete solution, many people are surprised of the many ways the system pays for itself and can make a big different to overall performance and profitability of organisations and sites.

To be properly matched to a boom gate or boom gate system does require specialised knowledge and experience.  If you are looking for a new boom gate system or upgrade and are wanting help selecting the best boom gate for your business or premises, [**get in touch here.**](https://www.parkezi.com.au/contact/)

We are Sydney-based boom gate experts, with a project team also assists with car park projects Australia-wide.

‍

# Smart Parking for Property Developers: Adding Value with Automation

When people think about modern property development, they usually picture architecture, landscaping or stylish interiors. But there’s one part of a project that often gets overlooked, even though it shapes the daily experience of residents, guests and staff. The car park.

Today’s buyers and tenants expect seamless, secure and low-touch parking. They want to drive in, park quickly and get on with their day without dealing with swipe cards or paper tickets. As a result, smart parking systems have become an essential feature for developers across Australia.

If you're planning a new residential, mixed-use or commercial project, here’s why automated parking technologies like Parkezi’s ANPR systems are becoming a real asset booster.

## Modern Developments Need Modern Access

Tenants and residents are used to the convenience of app-based services, mobile payments and automated entry systems. A traditional ticket or token-based car park feels outdated and often becomes a point of frustration.

Smart parking solutions meet modern expectations by giving residents:

- Seamless entry and exit through ANPR

- Access without swipe cards

- Contactless visitor management

- A smoother flow during busy times

For commercial properties, hotels and mixed-use buildings, these systems help staff, contractors and guests move quickly without bottlenecks at access points. Parkezi’s platforms are built to simplify movement while maintaining strong security.

## Improved Security and Controlled Access

Security is one of the biggest concerns for developers, especially in residential and mixed-use precincts. Car parks often become the weak point because swipe cards can be shared and traditional gates are easy to tailgate through.

ANPR access control offers a more secure alternative. Vehicles are granted access based on their licence plate, which means no physical passes to lose or copy. Developers can provide temporary access for:

- Visitors

- Delivery vehicles

- Maintenance teams

- Short-term tenants

Parkezi gives property managers a simple dashboard to manage permissions without needing to manually update cards or fobs.

## Higher Perceived Value for Buyers and Tenants

Smart parking technology is quickly becoming a selling feature rather than a behind-the-scenes upgrade. Buyers and tenants see automated parking as a sign that the building is modern, future-ready and built with their convenience in mind.

For developers, this can translate into:

- Faster leasing cycles

- Reduced vacancy

- Higher long-term property value

When residents know they can enter the building securely and without fuss, the entire living experience feels more premium.

## Smoother Operations for Property Managers

Once a development is complete, the long-term efficiency of the car park becomes a management responsibility. Automated parking systems make that much easier.

With smart management software, property teams can:

- View occupancy in real time

- Set or adjust access rules

- Monitor visitor activity

- Reduce manual traffic flow issues

- Respond to problems quickly

Parkezi’s cloud dashboard helps managers oversee the entire car park from a single interface. This is especially valuable for developers managing multi-site portfolios.

## Cost Savings Through Reduced Hardware and Maintenance

Traditional parking systems rely on bulky machines, magnetic readers and physical tickets. These systems are more expensive to maintain and require constant servicing.

Smart parking systems remove much of the physical hardware, which leads to:

- Less downtime

- Lower servicing costs

- Fewer moving parts

- No paper ticket waste

For developers, this translates to stronger long-term ROI and fewer headaches for property management teams.

## Better Traffic Flow in High-Density Projects

High-density developments come with predictable peak times. Morning exits, evening entries and weekend visitor traffic can quickly lead to congestion.

ANPR-based systems help smooth out these pressure points by maintaining steady entry and exit flow. Cars move through gates faster, which reduces the chances of queues spilling onto surrounding streets.

Parkezi’s systems are designed to handle high-traffic environments, making them ideal for apartments, shopping precincts, office buildings and mixed-use developments.

## Future-Ready for Electric Vehicles and Smart Precincts

As EV adoption grows in Australia, smart parking will play an important role in how developments manage charging bays and energy demand. ANPR and cloud management systems can integrate with EV solutions, making it easier to control who can access charging stations and when.

Developers who invest in smart parking early position their projects for:

- EV-ready infrastructure

- Smarter energy use

- Integration with broader smart-city technologies

Parkezi’s systems are built with this level of scalability in mind.

### FAQs

### 1. What is smart parking and how does it work in new developments?

Smart parking uses automated technology like licence plate recognition and cloud-based software to manage who can enter and exit a car park. Instead of swipe cards or paper tickets, vehicles are recognised automatically. For residents, staff and approved visitors, access just works in the background without extra steps.

### 2. Why are property developers choosing ANPR parking systems?

ANPR systems reduce friction for residents and tenants while improving security. There are no cards to lose or share, and access can be controlled remotely. From a developer point of view, it adds a modern, premium feel to the project and reduces long-term operational headaches after handover.

### 3. Does smart parking actually increase property value?

Yes, especially in competitive markets. Buyers and tenants increasingly expect seamless, low-touch access throughout a building. When parking is easy, secure and reliable, it lifts the overall perception of the development. That often leads to faster leasing, stronger tenant retention and better long-term asset value.

### 4. Is smart parking suitable for mixed-use and high-density developments?

It is one of the best fits. Mixed-use sites deal with residents, visitors, deliveries and staff all at once. Smart parking systems allow different access rules for each group and help manage peak traffic without congestion. This keeps surrounding streets clearer and makes daily movement more predictable.

### 5. How does smart parking help property managers after construction is complete?

Once the building is operational, smart parking gives managers visibility and control without manual work. They can update access permissions, monitor usage, and respond to issues through a single dashboard. This reduces ongoing costs and makes multi-site management far more efficient.

## Why Smart Parking Is a Must-Have for Modern Developments

Smart parking is no longer a nice-to-have. It’s becoming an essential piece of the property experience. Whether you’re developing apartments, hotels, office buildings or mixed-use spaces, automated parking helps improve safety, flow, value and overall tenant satisfaction.

If you are planning a new development or upgrading an existing one, now is a good time to think about how parking fits into the bigger experience of the building. The right system can make day-to-day life easier for residents, simplify management and add long-term value to the asset.

If you would like to explore how smart parking could work in your next project, talk with the team at Parkezi. We are happy to walk through your plans and show how automation can be built in from day one.

[**Get in touch**](https://www.parkezi.com.au/contact/)**with us here to start the conversation.**

post/the-future-of-parking-automation-in-australia article

Source body/lead retained with current links and shorter headings.

Original source · Redesigned route

Before: captured original

# The Future of Parking Automation in Australia

Parking technology in Australia has changed more in the last five years than it did in the previous twenty. From ticketless entry to mobile payments and Automated Number Plate Recognition (ANPR) cameras, our car parks are quickly becoming part of a wider ecosystem of smart, low-touch city technologies. As expectations shift and operators look for ways to work more efficiently, the move toward fully automated ANPR parking systems is strengthening.

So what does the future look like for parking automation in Australia? Let’s break down the key trends shaping the next wave of upgrades and improvements.

‍

## AI-Driven ANPR Is Becoming the New Normal

ANPR, or Automatic Number Plate Recognition, isn’t new, but its accuracy and speed have improved dramatically. With AI-enhanced recognition, cameras now read plates faster and in more difficult conditions, which makes entry and exit smoother for drivers.

In practical terms, this means a genuine drive-in, drive-out experience. No tickets. No tapping buttons. No winding down windows on a cold morning. The system simply recognises the number plate, checks permissions and opens the gate.

Parkezi already works with high-accuracy ANPR systems and cloud-based software that connects the entire parking journey. As more car parks upgrade to AI-assisted recognition, this style of low-touch access is quickly becoming what drivers expect everywhere they go.

‍

## Cloud Parking Management Is Taking Centre Stage

Traditional car park systems require local servers, on-site support and regular maintenance. Cloud parking management is changing that completely.

With cloud-connected systems, operators can monitor occupancy, revenue, entry logs and issues from anywhere. This helps facilities run more smoothly, especially multi-site portfolios like councils, shopping centres and mixed-use precincts.

Operators want to reduce overheads, and cloud software gives them control without needing staff on the ground for every issue. Parkezi’s cloud platform already supports real-time monitoring, remote diagnostics and frictionless access control, making it the backbone of modern parking operations. Unlike many other parking systems, with Parkezi, 100% of revenue goes direct to the carpark owner.

‍

## Contactless Payments Are Replacing Traditional Machines

Drivers are used to tapping their phones, watches and cards for almost everything. Parking is no different. Contactless payments and mobile checkout options are steadily replacing bulky pay stations that cost thousands to maintain and often break down at the worst time.

This shift means:

- QR codes and tap-and-go have become standard payment methods

- Mobile receipt delivery replaces paper based receipts

- Faster exits and fewer disputes

When drivers have the option to pay on their phone or automatically through their licence plate, there’s no need to stop at a payment machine at all.

Parkezi’s Express Pay and Scan & Go systems are designed for this transition, allowing operators multiple options to collect revenue without slowing down the customer experience.

‍

## Data-Driven Pricing Improves Revenue

Parking pricing in Australia is starting to resemble airline and rideshare models. With better data on peak periods, repeat visits and dwell times, operators can adjust pricing in a way that’s fair to customers while maximising occupancy.

More operators now use:

- Demand-based pricing

- Cheaper off-peak periods

- Event-based tariff adjustments

- Discounts for short stays

ANPR systems paired with cloud software give operators accurate insights, helping them make decisions based on real usage rather than assumptions. Parkezi’s reporting tools support this kind of data-led planning, making it easier for operators to improve turnover and reduce congestion.

‍

## Hardware Is Becoming Lighter, Greener and Easier to Maintain

Car parks used to rely on big, mechanical machines that jammed, wore out and needed constant servicing. Today’s technology is cleaner and more efficient with fewer moving parts, lower energy use and reduced paper waste

This shift also supports environmental targets by reducing idle time, cutting emissions and improving air quality in busy areas.

Parkezi installs next-generation hardware that pairs seamlessly with cloud platforms, which means less downtime and a smoother experience for drivers.

‍

## Privacy and Compliance Remain a Priority

As Australia moves deeper into data-driven parking systems, privacy matters more than ever. Operators must handle licence plate data responsibly and comply with the Australian Privacy Act.

Parkezi’s systems follow privacy-by-design principles, ensuring both operators and visitors feel confident that their data is handled correctly.

## Preparing for the Future Today

The future of parking automation is already here. From ANPR access to cloud-based dashboards and contactless payments, Australian operators are moving toward smarter, cleaner and more efficient systems.

If you’re looking to modernise your car park, Parkezi can help you make the shift with practical, easy-to-use technology that improves both customer experience and operational performance. [Contact us today.](https://www.parkezi.com.au/contact)

Parking automation ahead
Parking automation ahead
24 November 2025
Parking technology in Australia has changed more in the last five years than it did in the previous twenty. From ticketless entry to mobile payments and Automated Number Plate Recognition (ANPR) cameras, our car parks are quickly becoming part of a wider ecosystem of smart, low-touch city technologies. As expectations shift and operators look for ways to work more efficiently, the move toward fully automated ANPR parking systems is strengthening.
So what does the future look like for parking automation in Australia? Let’s break down the key trends shaping the next wave of upgrades and improvements.
AI-Driven ANPR Is Becoming the New Normal
ANPR, or Automatic Number Plate Recognition, isn’t new, but its accuracy and speed have improved dramatically. With AI-enhanced recognition, cameras now read plates faster and in more difficult conditions, which makes entry and exit smoother for drivers.
In practical terms, this means a genuine drive-in, drive-out experience. No tickets. No tapping buttons. No winding down windows on a cold morning. The system simply recognises the number plate, checks permissions and opens the gate.
Parkezi already works with high-accuracy ANPR systems and cloud-based software that connects the entire parking journey. As more car parks upgrade to AI-assisted recognition, this style of low-touch access is quickly becoming what drivers expect everywhere they go.
Cloud parking management
Traditional car park systems require local servers, on-site support and regular maintenance. Cloud parking management is changing that completely.
With cloud-connected systems, operators can monitor occupancy, revenue, entry logs and issues from anywhere. This helps facilities run more smoothly, especially multi-site portfolios like councils, shopping centres and mixed-use precincts.
Operators want to reduce overheads, and cloud software gives them control without needing staff on the ground for every issue. Parkezi’s cloud platform already supports real-time monitoring, remote diagnostics and frictionless access control, making it the backbone of modern parking operations. Unlike many other parking systems, with Parkezi, 100% of revenue goes direct to the carpark owner.
Contactless payments
Drivers are used to tapping their phones, watches and cards for almost everything. Parking is no different. Contactless payments and mobile checkout options are steadily replacing bulky pay stations that cost thousands to maintain and often break down at the worst time.
This shift means:
QR codes and tap-and-go have become standard payment methods
Mobile receipt delivery replaces paper based receipts
Faster exits and fewer disputes
When drivers have the option to pay on their phone or automatically through their licence plate, there’s no need to stop at a payment machine at all.
Parkezi’s Express Pay and Scan & Go systems are designed for this transition, allowing operators multiple options to collect revenue without slowing down the customer experience.
Data-Driven Pricing Improves Revenue
Parking pricing in Australia is starting to resemble airline and rideshare models. With better data on peak periods, repeat visits and dwell times, operators can adjust pricing in a way that’s fair to customers while maximising occupancy.
More operators now use:
Demand-based pricing
Cheaper off-peak periods
Event-based tariff adjustments
Discounts for short stays
ANPR systems paired with cloud software give operators accurate insights, helping them make decisions based on real usage rather than assumptions. Parkezi’s reporting tools support this kind of data-led planning, making it easier for operators to improve turnover and reduce congestion.
Parking hardware is changing
Car parks used to rely on big, mechanical machines that jammed, wore out and needed constant servicing. Today’s technology is cleaner and more efficient with fewer moving parts, lower energy use and reduced paper waste
This shift also supports environmental targets by reducing idle time, cutting emissions and improving air quality in busy areas.
Parkezi installs next-generation hardware that pairs seamlessly with cloud platforms, which means less downtime and a smoother experience for drivers.
Privacy and Compliance Remain a Priority
As Australia moves deeper into data-driven parking systems, privacy matters more than ever. Operators must handle licence plate data responsibly and comply with the Australian Privacy Act.
Parkezi’s systems follow privacy-by-design principles, ensuring both operators and visitors feel confident that their data is handled correctly.
Preparing for the Future Today
The future of parking automation is already here. From ANPR access to cloud-based dashboards and contactless payments, Australian operators are moving toward smarter, cleaner and more efficient systems.
If you’re looking to modernise your car park, Parkezi can help you make the shift with practical, easy-to-use technology that improves both customer experience and operational performance.
Contact us today.

# What to look for in an Automatic Number Plate Recognition (Parkezi) system

Who would have ever imagined that the presence of Automatic Number Plate Recognition (Parkezi) technology and cameras in Australia would have expanded so rapidly and extensively?

Although the first thought that springs to mind for most people is shopping centre car parks, the possibilities for Parkezi are in fact endless.

Here in Australian businesses, vehicles need to get in, be loaded and get out to where they need to be.   Customers and staff are busier than ever and are often competing for limited parking spaces.  Even homeowners want fast and hassle-free entry into home driveways without having to scramble for clickers and remotes.  This is where Parkezi comes into its own.

Parkezi has completely changed how we move around and do business and has given car park operators the tools to improve car park and driveway traffic efficiency.  It can also help

- reduce operating costs and

- make it simpler, and more likely, for people to engage with your organisation or facility.

Like most technology, things can change overnight, so when choosing an Parkezi system for your car park or driveway, it needs to have the advancements and reliability that will service you into the future.

We’ve seen it time and again when people have unwittingly gone for an inferior Parkezi option later to find it was a complete waste of their time and money.

To make sure that doesn’t happen to you, we recommend you always go for a car park solution that’s designed and installed by experts who will consider your needs now and as you grow, and one that will provide longevity.

When choosing an Parkezi supplier, here are some questions you may want to keep in mind when looking for the right Parkezi equipment and system for your needs.

‍

## How accurate are Parkezi cameras?

If your APNR-Optical Character Recognition (OCR) camera and software does not have high levels of accuracy you could be in for a whole lot of pain trying to sort out errors and confusion.

Here at Smart Parking Solutions, we only use leading product brands known for their quality and accuracy, such as Selea.  Selea has an Parkezi-optical character recognition (OCR) camera and software, for instance, that can deliver extraordinary levels of accuracy of up to **99.8%.**

What’s more surprising, the best performing cameras are often more compact, robust and affordable than you may expect so it’s a good idea to assess the bigger picture benefits.

‍

## Do Parkezi-OCR cameras read number plates if dirty or damaged?

Let’s face it.  In Australia’s conditions, there’s going to be times drivers find themselves in a bit of mud or strife causing damage to their number plate.  The great news is, there are cameras and software that do a surprising job at reading plates through dirt so make sure the equipment you choose has that capability.

The optical power and software capabilities of a Selea, as one example, are so advanced even tricksters who think they can hide the identity of their vehicles with a smear of dirt may be in for a surprise.

‍

## What is the best APNR solution for your situation?

Try to stay clear of the cowboys.   You’re going to be getting a better fitting solution from an expert who takes the time to understand your needs and has the experience to point you in the direction of solutions that will give you outstanding value and performance.

‍

## What about installation?

Find out who is going to be installing your system.  Proper installation is key to a system operating as it should so really does need to be left to professionals, preferably ones that understand the hardware and software inside and out.

‍

## Is maintenance required?

Although the Parkezi system is automated, for optimal performance you will need some degree of maintenance and upkeep.

Parkezi experts, like Smart Parking Solutions, will be able to explain what you can do yourself and make recommendations for when maintenance by a trained technician is needed.  You may even want to ask them about maintenance plans so they can take care of scheduling for you.

‍

## How to find out more.

If you would like to find out more about automatic number plate recognition technology and how it can make a positive difference to your business, residential premises or facility, [**contact our expert team**](https://www.parkezi.com.au/contact/) today.

# Why Automatic Number Plate Recognition (Anpr) Carpark Access Systems Make Sense for Strata Managers and Residents Alike

Automatic Number Plate Recognition technology has come a long way in the last 5 years.

‍

Parkezi systems (also known as Licence Plate Recognition systems) are more robust and affordable than they have ever been which is why you have probably seen that most shopping centre carparks are now using them.

But what about residential complexes and apartment carparks?

Increasingly, strata managers are upgrading traditional carpark access systems to Parkezi which is proving to be a win for them and the tenants in their buildings.  It provides streamlined access for those that belong and makes it harder for those that don’t.

For tenants the other benefits of having access to a properly designed Parkezi system include:

- Improved building security as there is captured video footage of vehicles entering and exiting the carpark giving them greater peace of mind

- Access to a tenant car park portal which allows for easier remote gate opening and easy DIY updating of vehicle details.  Booking guest parking is also so much simpler.

- The need to manage *(and replace!)* expensive access keys is eliminated

For strata managers, the right Parkezi system makes managing car parks less stressful and equips them with data that assists with planning processes, decision making and efficient car park operations, such as:

·       Understanding peak traffic times

·       Recognising where car spaces are underutilised

·       Levels of tenant, guest and other visitor traffic, such as tradespeople

·       Identify good times for carpark maintenance and upgrades, and

·       Simplify vehicle authentication, including spotting which vehicles may be overstaying their welcome.

The technology can also help with reporting by exception so the building manager is alerted to specific incidents.  And as tenants have access to a tenant car park portal, you can expect a reduced number of panicked middle-of-the-night phone calls to building security to arrange access!

The effectiveness of the Parkezi system, however, is dictated by the quality of the carpark camera, the integration of software and other equipment such as boom and sliding gates, and the installation of the carpark system itself.

Here at **Smart Parking Solutions**, for instance, we are **Parkezi experts**, and can provide **Parkezi cameras** with an **accuracy level of up to 99.8%,** including a surprising level of capability to read dirty and damaged number plates.  What this means is less errors and less hassles for you.

When choosing a Parkezi system provider, also consider your training and maintenance needs.  Does the Parkezi camera and carpark access system provider offer maintenance plans to help **keep your car park running efficiently**?

To learn more about how Automatic Number Plate Recognition technology can revolutionise your strata carpark operations [**call the Smart Parking Solutions Parkezi team today**](https://www.parkezi.com.au/contact/).

‍

post/improve-parking-revenue-without-increasing-fees article

Before: captured original

# How to Improve Parking Revenue Without Increasing Fees

When parking revenue falls short of expectations, raising fees can seem like the obvious solution. Charge a little more per vehicle and overall revenue should increase.

In practice, it is not always that simple.

Higher fees can frustrate regular users and make alternative parking more attractive. For some sites, increasing prices can even affect demand.

Before changing the fee structure, there is another question worth asking: is the car park collecting all the revenue it should already be generating?

Lost tickets, unpaid exits, manual overrides, payment friction and inefficient traffic flow can quietly reduce revenue every day. Across hundreds of transactions, relatively small problems can add up quickly.

For many operators, the opportunity is not to charge more. It is to make the existing parking operation work better.

## Key Insight

Improving parking revenue is not simply about how much you charge. It is about accurately capturing legitimate transactions, making parking easy to use and ensuring the car park can efficiently handle the demand already there.

## Start by Finding Where Revenue Is Being Lost

Revenue leakage is not always easy to see.

Traditional parking systems often depend on paper tickets, payment machines and manual intervention. Each additional step creates another opportunity for something to go wrong.

Common issues include:

- Lost or damaged tickets

- Manual boom gate overrides

- Vehicles leaving without completing payment

- Faulty payment equipment

- Inaccurate entry and exit records

- Unauthorised access

One missed payment may not seem significant. Repeated across a busy commercial car park, however, the impact can become substantial.

The bigger problem is visibility. If the system does not provide reliable vehicle and transaction data, operators may know revenue is below expectations without knowing exactly where the problem sits.

## How ANPR Helps Reduce Revenue Leakage

Automatic Number Plate Recognition, or ANPR, replaces the paper ticket with a digital vehicle record.

When a vehicle enters, its number plate is recorded. When it leaves, the system matches the plate with its parking session.

There is no physical ticket to lose, damage or transfer between vehicles. Entry and exit times are also recorded digitally, giving operators a clearer picture of what is happening within the car park.

For casual parking facilities, that additional visibility can make revenue collection more accountable.

But reducing leakage is only part of the opportunity.

Making parking easier can also encourage more people to use it.

## Knight Frank Chatswood: A Real Revenue Example

The Knight Frank managed car park in Chatswood shows how changing the parking process can affect both revenue collection and demand.

The facility has 120 bays, with 50 leased bays on the top floor and 70 casual bays. There are also 18 tenant companies, each with access to its own login.

Before the upgrade, casual parkers used paper tickets while tenants with leased bays used swipe cards.

The system had a significant weakness. People were standing near the exit gate and allowing casual parkers to leave without paying. There was no reliable way to stop it or even see how frequently it was occurring.

Parkezi replaced the setup with a fully ticketless, paperless ANPR system.

After the change, casual transactions increased from around 50 per day to approximately 140 per day.

Monthly casual parking revenue also increased from around $8,000 to approximately $20,000, meaning it more than doubled. At those volumes, the difference represents roughly $144,000 a year in additional casual parking revenue.

Interestingly, average revenue per transaction fell slightly while transaction volume nearly tripled.

That suggests the result was not simply about recovering unpaid parking.

The site operates casual parking as a four-hour, high-turnover product. Making the experience ticketless and allowing drivers to pay by phone appears to have made the car park more attractive for shorter visits as well.

Someone parking for 40 minutes, for example, may be far more willing to use a car park when they know they will not have to collect a ticket, find a payment machine and complete another manual process before leaving.

That is an important lesson for operators. Sometimes making parking easier can generate more value than simply making it more expensive.

## Faster Exits Matter Too

Traffic flow is usually discussed as a customer experience issue, but it can also influence parking performance.

At Knight Frank Chatswood, the difference in exit times is clear.

Before the upgrade, exiting took around 45 seconds per vehicle.

With the new system, a driver paying by card at the gate takes around 20 seconds.

For someone who has already paid by phone, exit is effectively instant. The ANPR camera reads the plate and the boom gate lifts without the driver stopping.

Across a single vehicle, that saving may seem small. Across dozens of vehicles leaving during a busy period, it can make a noticeable difference.

Shorter exit times reduce queues and help a high-turnover car park process demand more efficiently.

## Make Payment Easier, Not More Expensive

Drivers are used to quick digital payments in almost every other part of their day. Parking should not feel unnecessarily complicated.

Depending on the site, modern parking systems can offer:

- Tap-and-go payments

- Mobile payments

- QR code payments

- Online payment

- Plate-linked payment processes

The goal is not simply to offer more technology. It is to remove unnecessary steps between parking and payment.

The Chatswood example demonstrates the value of this approach. Phone payment does not just provide another payment method. It changes the exit experience because prepaid vehicles can leave as soon as their plate is recognised.

For high-turnover sites, reducing this type of friction can support both customer experience and commercial performance.

## Use Parking Data to Make Better Decisions

Modern parking systems also give operators better visibility over what is happening on site.

Depending on the configuration, this can include:

- Transaction volumes

- Vehicle entries and exits

- Occupancy patterns

- Peak periods

- Dwell times

- Payment activity

This information helps answer a much more useful question than simply, "Should we increase our fees?"

It helps operators understand why revenue is performing the way it is.

A car park may have a pricing problem, but it may instead have a collection problem, a turnover issue or unnecessary payment friction.

Reliable data helps separate one from the other.

## Look Beyond Revenue Per Transaction

One of the strongest lessons from the Knight Frank project is that average transaction value should not be viewed in isolation.

At Chatswood, the average amount generated per transaction fell slightly. Yet transaction volume nearly tripled and monthly casual revenue more than doubled.

For operators, this highlights the value of looking at the complete parking journey.

If a car park becomes easier to enter, pay for and exit, it may attract more short-stay users and improve turnover. That can ultimately be more valuable than trying to maximise the amount earned from every individual vehicle.

The better question may be:

How can we make the car park easier for more legitimate customers to use?

## Key Takeaways

- Raising parking fees is not the only way to improve revenue.

- Revenue can be lost through unpaid exits, manual overrides and inefficient ticket-based processes.

- ANPR creates a digital vehicle record and improves transaction visibility.

- At Knight Frank Chatswood, casual transactions increased from around 50 to 140 per day.

- Monthly casual revenue increased from around $8,000 to $20,000, meaning it more than doubled.

- Exit times reduced from around 45 seconds to approximately 20 seconds for card payments, while prepaid phone users can exit without stopping.

- Improving convenience can support transaction volume as well as revenue collection.

## Related Reading

Learn more about how Parkezi's [parking automation systems](https://www.parkezi.com.au/systems) combine ANPR, access control and digital parking management.

If you are considering upgrading an existing car park, see Parkezi's approach to [parking system installation](https://www.parkezi.com.au/installation).

You can also explore the [Knight Frank Chatswood project](https://www.parkezi.com.au/project/knight-frank-chatswood) for more information about the site.

## Frequently Asked Questions

### Can parking revenue increase without raising fees?

Yes. Operators can improve revenue by reducing leakage, increasing legitimate transaction volumes, improving turnover and making payment easier.

### How does ANPR help reduce lost parking revenue?

ANPR links the parking session to the vehicle's number plate instead of a paper ticket. This provides a clearer digital record of entry and exit activity and reduces issues associated with lost or transferred tickets.

### Can faster exits affect parking revenue?

For high-turnover sites, they can. Faster exits reduce congestion and allow vehicles to move through the facility more efficiently, while also improving the customer experience.

### Is revenue per transaction the most important parking metric?

Not necessarily. At Knight Frank Chatswood, average revenue per transaction fell slightly, but transaction volume nearly tripled and monthly casual revenue more than doubled. Overall utilisation and transaction volume can be just as important.

## Getting More From Your Existing Car Park

Improving parking revenue does not always require charging customers more.

Often, the better opportunity is to identify where revenue is being lost, remove unnecessary friction and make the car park easier for legitimate customers to use.

Knight Frank Chatswood demonstrates what that can look like. Moving from paper tickets to ticketless ANPR helped address unpaid exits, increased casual transactions and contributed to monthly casual revenue more than doubling.

If you want to understand where your existing parking operation could perform better, [contact Parkezi](https://www.parkezi.com.au/contact) to discuss ANPR, ticketless parking and digital payment options for your site.

post/why-sydney-property-managers-are-investing-in-parking-automation article
